# ITR-2 CBDT Validation Rule Gap Mapping — AY 2026-27

**Purpose:** a rule-by-rule cross-reference of every official CBDT ITR-2 Validation Rule against
the current codebase (`app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py`, `calc_rules.py`, the
calculator, the CBDT JSON builder, and the canonical schemas), to find every genuine gap before
planning implementation work. This document supersedes `Docs/ITR2_CBDT_VALIDATION_RULE_MATRIX.md`
for the purpose of implementation planning — that earlier draft's rule text and classifications
should not be relied on going forward (see §0).

**Companion document:** `Docs/ITR2_FRONTEND_AND_SERIALIZATION_AUDIT_AY2026_27.md` §22 covers the
live E2E functional audit (CBDT JSON generation, Tax Computation wiring, PDF download) that
preceded this validator-specific pass. That audit found the JSON-generation *pipeline itself* has
active bugs independent of validator coverage (§22.2-§22.4) — those are ITD-builder/filing-gateway
defects, not validator gaps, and are tracked there, not here.

**Source:** Official CBDT PDF `Reference Docs by CBDT & ITD/Official Validations/
CBDT__e-Filing_ITR 2_Validation Rules_AY 2026-27_V1.0 (1).pdf` (51 pages), re-extracted directly
(see §0 for why) — not the pre-existing `tmp/cbdt_rules/*.txt` flat dump.

**Inventory:** 764 Category A rules + 26 Category B/D rules = **790 rules** (count independently
confirmed via the corrected extraction in §0, matching the original draft's own count — the total
was right, only many of the individual rows' text and boundaries were wrong).

---

## 0. Why this document exists instead of just updating the old matrix

`Docs/ITR2_CBDT_VALIDATION_RULE_MATRIX.md` (831 lines, pre-existing) has a structural defect in
its own source data, discovered while starting this re-verification pass: **the PDF-to-text
extraction it was built from has row-boundary drift** — text from one official rule's sentence
regularly bleeds into the *next* row, and the following row is then missing its own true opening
clause. This is not a one-off glitch; it recurs throughout the document. Concrete example, taken
directly from the old draft:

> Old draft, row 27: *"In Schedule S, Sl. No. 6 Income chargeable under Salaries should be output
> of Sl. No 4-5 Exemption u/s 10(10) for gratuity shall not exceed income offered under the head
> salary under sub head gratuity receipt and cannot exceed"* — this is actually **two different
> rules concatenated**: the true rule #27 is only the first sentence; the rest is the *opening* of
> rule #28.
>
> Old draft, row 28: *"(i) Rs20 lakhs in case of Nature of employment is... (ii)Rs 25 lakhs in
> case of... If old tax regime is selected, In Schedule S in Field 3 dropdown value of 'Sec
> 10(13A)-'"* — this row's own middle is rule #28's real continuation, but its *tail* is actually
> the opening of rule #29, not part of #28 at all.

Root cause: the PDF renders each rule as a numbered two-column table row (`Sl. No.` | `Scenario`),
but for any row whose scenario text wraps across multiple lines, the "Sl. No." digit is rendered
**vertically centered relative to that row's own multi-line text block**, not aligned to its first
line. A naive linear text extraction (which both the old draft's `tmp/cbdt_rules/*.txt` dump and
even `pdfplumber`'s own default `extract_text()` produce) reads the number as if it always marks
the *start* of a row, which is only true for single-line rows — for any multi-line row, 1-2 lines
of the *previous* rule's tail get attributed to the wrong side of the number, corrupting both rows.
This was confirmed by inspecting the PDF's own word bounding boxes directly (rule #2 spans two
text lines at `top=183.7` and `top=197.5`; its own "2." marker sits at `top=190.7` — between them,
not at the start).

**Fix applied for this document:** re-extracted the entire PDF using `pdfplumber`'s word-level
bounding boxes, splitting the *Scenario* column's own line stream into rows by vertical line-gap
size instead of trusting the number column's position at all — within a wrapped paragraph,
consecutive lines are reliably ~12-14pt apart; between two distinct table rows, the gap is
reliably ~22-32pt (confirmed via histogram across a 10-page sample: 167 gaps clustered at 22.0pt,
140 at 14.0pt, a clean bimodal split with no ambiguous middle). This is immune to the number
column's centering, because it never looks at the number column's vertical position to decide
where one rule ends and the next begins. Result: all 764 Category A rows and 26 Category B/D rows
now read as complete, grammatically coherent, standalone sentences — spot-checked across the full
range (rows 1-10, 26-35, 115, 143, 229, 251, 282, 655, 745-764, and all 26 B/D rows) with zero
remaining drift found. One inherited ambiguity from the source PDF itself (not an extraction
artifact) is noted in §Category B/D, row 20 — a sentence that appears genuinely truncated in the
official PDF's own layout, reproduced identically by three independent extraction methods.

**Practical consequence:** every rule's "Official CBDT scenario" text in this document is the
freshly re-extracted, verified text — not copied from the old draft. Any prior classification work
done against the old draft's garbled text (including any row where the old draft's own scenario
column doesn't match a real, single, coherent official rule) needed to be redone from scratch
against the corrected text, which is what §1-§3 below are.

---

## Status taxonomy

Independently re-derived for this pass (not inherited from the old draft) — matching CLAUDE.md's
own explicit guidance on when a CBDT validation rule is genuinely worth a dedicated check versus
already structurally guaranteed or out of this repo's scope:

1. **Explicit validator executed** — a real runtime validator function in `input_rules.py` or
   `calc_rules.py` checks exactly this scenario, cited by exact rule-ID constant and file:line,
   with a test citation where one exists.
2. **Indirect calculator/schema/builder coverage — proof pending** — the value is structurally
   guaranteed by how it's computed or built (e.g. a JSON total is `sum(...)` in the builder
   itself), cited by exact file:line, but no direct test proves the specific CBDT scenario.
3. **Schema-level invariant** — a Pydantic `@model_validator`/`Field` constraint already makes the
   bad state impossible to construct, cited by exact file:line.
4. **Missing — genuine gap** — the scenario is real, the data is user-suppliable and consumed
   downstream, and no code path enforces it. This is the only status that feeds Phase 3 planning.
5. **Not representable / out of scope** — the field doesn't exist in the canonical schema, or the
   check is between a CBDT dropdown-UI's own sub-fields that this repo's field-driven (not
   UI-summing) architecture makes structurally moot — explained concretely per row, not asserted.
6. **External dependency / not locally verifiable** — requires ITD portal state, another
   taxpayer's own return, or Aadhaar/PAN database lookup.
7. **(Category B/D only) Warning not emitted — advisory only** — a real, non-blocking advisory
   scenario with no code path emitting any warning-level message for it at all.

Quality bar: no "~" placeholder evidence. A citation is either an exact file:line the reviewer
actually confirmed contains the claimed logic, or the row is honestly marked "Missing."

---

## Summary of findings (790/790 rules independently re-derived)

| Status | Count | % |
|---|---:|---:|
| Indirect calculator/schema/builder coverage — proof pending | 348 | 44.1% |
| Missing — genuine gap | 175 | 22.2% |
| Explicit validator executed | 115 | 14.6% |
| Not representable / out of scope | 97 | 12.3% |
| Schema-level invariant | 23 | 2.9% |
| Warning not emitted — advisory only (Category B/D) | 17 | 2.2% |
| External dependency / not locally verifiable | 15 | 1.9% |
| **Total** | **790** | **100%** |

Reading this honestly (per this document's own quality bar, not to make the number look better or
worse than it is):

- **175 genuine gaps (22.2%)** is the number that matters for Phase 3 implementation planning —
  real, user-suppliable scenarios with no enforcement anywhere in the pipeline today.
- **348 "indirect coverage" rows (44.1%)** are the bulk of the inventory and are mostly *not*
  implementation work — they are arithmetic-identity rules (`Sl.No X = sum of Sl.No Y+Z`) that this
  codebase's own architecture (building JSON from typed calculator output, not summing a raw
  editable UI) already makes true by construction. The "proof pending" qualifier means a
  **regression test** would be valuable, not that a **validator** is missing — conflating the two
  would inflate Phase 3's scope with low-value test-writing dressed up as gap-closing. A smaller
  number of these (flagged individually in the tables below, e.g. rows 116-146's "not fully traced
  this pass" notes) genuinely need a follow-up code read before their classification can be
  fully trusted — noted honestly rather than asserted with false confidence.
- **97 "not representable" rows (12.3%)** are dominated by one large structural cluster: ~49+48=97
  Schedule EI "same dropdown selected twice" rules (rows ~649-697 and 698-745) are *all* moot
  because `ExemptIncome` (`app/schemas/itr2.py:512-521`) models Schedule EI as six fixed scalar
  fields plus one generic catch-all, never a repeatable per-section dropdown row list — the
  official form's own UI-duplication failure mode cannot occur in a schema shaped this way. This
  single architectural fact resolves nearly half of the "not representable" bucket at once; it is
  not 97 independent judgment calls.
- **115 explicit + 23 schema-invariant (138 total, 17.5%)** are the rules with the strongest
  existing evidence — a real validator function or a Pydantic constructor-time guarantee, most with
  exact rule-ID citations.
- **15 external-dependency rows (1.9%)** need ITD portal/PAN-database/another-taxpayer's-return
  state this codebase cannot access standalone — correctly out of scope for a local validator.

**Twenty-one additional, previously-undocumented bugs** were found incidentally while reading the
validator/calculator/builder code for this pass (not part of the requested rule-by-rule mapping,
flagged only, not fixed) — see §Additional bugs noticed at the end of this document. Two are
severe enough to warrant calling out here: **Schedule HP's `PassThroghIncome` line is hardcoded to
zero** even when the calculator correctly includes PTI house-property income in the schedule's own
total (a live "right total, wrong line item" defect), and the **AMT/AMTC credit chain is largely
unwired into Part B-TTI** (several fields hardcoded to `0`, and current-year AMT credit generation
is never computed at all) — both are ITD-builder correctness bugs, not validator gaps, but were
found only because this pass required reading that code closely.

---

## Category A rules (764)

| # | Official CBDT scenario (verbatim) | Status | Exact evidence | Rule ID / notes |
|---:|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Assessee should enter valid Mobile Number | Schema-level invariant | `app/schemas/itr1.py:1432-1433` `FilingAddress.mobile_no: str = Field(pattern=r"^[1-9][0-9]{4,9}$")`, used by `ITR2FilingProfile.primary_address` (`app/schemas/itr2.py:177`) | Cannot construct an `ITR2FilingProfile` with a malformed mobile number. |
| 2 | Name of the taxpayer does not match with the "Name" as per the PAN database... | External dependency / not locally verifiable | — | Requires a live ITD/PAN-database name lookup; no such data source exists in this codebase. |
| 3 | Assessee claiming benefit of senior/super-senior citizen, but DOB not matching PAN/Aadhaar database | External dependency / not locally verifiable | — | `age_bracket` (`ITR2Input.age_bracket`) is self-declared; cross-checking it against the PAN/Aadhaar database requires live external data this codebase cannot query. |
| 4 | If original return filed u/s 142(1), revised return cannot be filed | Not representable / out of scope | `app/schemas/itr2.py:226-289` (`ITR2FilingProfile.validate_conditional_filing_facts`) | The schema has no field capturing the *original* return's own filing section — only the current return's `return_file_section`. Checking this needs new schema work (tracking the original return's section), not just a new validator function. |
| 5 | "held unlisted equity shares" = Yes → details should be filled | Schema-level invariant | `app/schemas/itr2.py:251-252`: `if self.held_unlisted_equity and not self.unlisted_equity_entries: raise ValueError(...)` | Constructing the bad state raises `ValidationError` at model construction. |
| 6 | Portuguese Civil Code s.5A = Yes → Schedule 5A mandatory | Schema-level invariant | `app/schemas/itr2.py:1157-1158` (`ITR2Input.validate_cross_schedule_contract`): `if self.filing_profile.portuguese_civil_code_applies != (self.schedule_5a is not None): raise ValueError(...)` | Two-way check — also covers rule #14 (Portuguese Civil Code = No → Schedule 5A must not be filed), since it's an equality check, not one-directional. |
| 7 | "filed by a representative assessee" = Yes → details provided | Schema-level invariant | `app/schemas/itr2.py:269-270`: `if self.verification_capacity == "R" and self.assessee_representative is None: raise ValueError(...)` | Also the converse at line 271-272 (representative details present but capacity isn't "R"). |
| 8 | Representative Assessee filing, but PAN in Schedule Verification ≠ PAN of uploader | External dependency / not locally verifiable | `app/schemas/itr2.py:150-157` (`AssesseeRepresentativeProfile`) has no PAN field at all | The "PAN who is trying to upload the return" is an ERI/portal login-session identity fact, not data this codebase holds or can compare against pre-submission. |
| 9 | Seventh proviso to s.139(1) = Yes → respective details filled | Explicit validator executed + Schema-level invariant | `app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py:109-118` (`ITR2-IN-PROFILE-003`); plus per-field minimums at `app/schemas/itr2.py:259-264` | Dual coverage: the validator checks "at least one amount > 0"; the schema separately enforces each flag's own statutory minimum amount. |
| 10 | "Director in a company" = Yes → respective details filled | Schema-level invariant | `app/schemas/itr2.py:249-250`: `if self.is_company_director and not self.company_director_entries: raise ValueError(...)` | — |

## Category B/D rules (26, advisory)

Category B/D rules are advisory warnings, not blocking errors (Category B: return uploads but the
taxpayer is warned of a possible 139(9) defect; Category D: return uploads, supporting documents
may be required — `app/engine/validators/base.py:15-19`). The warning mechanism itself already
exists in this codebase: `_result()` (`app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py:30-48`) accepts a
`severity` parameter, and `ITR2-IN-FORM-001`/`ITR2-IN-FORM-002`
(`app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py:1009-1022`) already emit non-blocking `Severity.D`
advisories exactly matching this pattern for rules #5/#6 below.

| # | Official CBDT scenario (verbatim) | Status | Exact evidence | Rule ID / notes |
|---:|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Form 29C- Report under section 115JC is mandatory to file if AMT tax is more than Normal Tax | Warning not emitted — advisory only | `result.amt_tax` and slab/normal tax are both already computed (`app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:1103-1109`), and the `Severity.D` mechanism is proven in production use (`ITR2-IN-FORM-001`/`002`) — but no rule anywhere in `app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py` or `calc_rules.py` compares them to emit a Form-29C reminder. | Real, checkable scenario; mechanism exists; not wired up. |
| 2 | Total income under section 115JC ≤ Rs. 50,00,000 and surcharge is entered in Schedule B-TTI | Warning not emitted — advisory only | `result.surcharge` and `amt.adjusted_total_income` are both available (`app/engine/calculators/itr2.py`, `app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py:1037-1052`); no advisory checks this combination. | Same — data exists, no warning emitted. |
| 3 | If the assessee is claiming relief u/s 90 & 91 then it is mandatory to file form 67 | Warning not emitted — advisory only | `TR1Entry.form67_filed: bool = False` (`app/schemas/itr2.py:570`) already captures exactly the fact this rule needs; no rule reads it to warn when `relief_claimed > 0 and not form67_filed`. | Field already exists specifically for this purpose and is unused for warning. |
| 4 | If assessee is shown income under section 115BBF then furnishing of Form 3CFA is mandatory within due time | Warning not emitted — advisory only | `ScheduleSIEntry.section` includes `"115BBF"` as a valid literal (`app/schemas/itr2.py:490`) and the calculator specifically branches on it (`app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:929`) — the underlying scenario is fully representable; no Form-3CFA advisory exists. | Data exists, no warning emitted. |
| 5 | Form 10E is required to be filed to claim Relief u/s 89 | Explicit validator executed | `app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py:1009-1015` (rule `ITR2-IN-FORM-001`, `Severity.D`). Test: `tests/test_itr2_input_validation.py:888,895`. | Explicitly implemented exactly as this rule requires. |
| 6 | Form 10BA is required to be filed to claim deduction u/s 80GG | Explicit validator executed | `app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py:1016-1022` (rule `ITR2-IN-FORM-002`, `Severity.D`). Test: `tests/test_itr2_input_validation.py:901,908`. | Explicitly implemented exactly as this rule requires. |
| 7 | In Schedule CYLA, the whole OS loss should be set off against any head of income in case income is more than loss; losses not applied cannot be carried forward | Indirect calculator/schema/builder coverage — proof pending | `app/engine/schedules/loss_setoff/cyla.py` allocates each loss basket (including OS-linked HP loss via `hp_setoff`, lines 269-280) greedily against every available positive-income pool up to its statutory ceiling before returning — the algorithm cannot leave a fully-absorbable loss unset-off. | Algorithmic guarantee rather than an explicit advisory rule; no CBDT-numbered test. |
| 8 | For Resident taxpayers, DTAA benefit is not available in rate of taxation; residents may claim DTAA benefit under Schedule TR and FSI | Warning not emitted — advisory only | `ResidentialStatus.RESIDENT` is available on every input (`app/schemas/itr2.py`), and special-rate/DTAA claims exist in `si_entries`/Schedule OS — the scenario (a resident using a DTAA-preferential rate outside Schedule TR/FSI) is representable but no advisory checks it; only the *opposite* direction (FSI/TR schedules disallowed for non-residents, `ITR2-IN-FSI-003`/`ITR2-IN-TR1-003`, both `Severity.A` blocking) is implemented. | Data exists; the implemented rules cover a different (blocking) scenario, not this advisory one. |
| 9 | TDS credited in hands of other person will be allowed to taxpayer only if that other person declares the same in schedule TDS of their ITR | External dependency / not locally verifiable | Requires visibility into a *different* taxpayer's own filed ITR/TDS schedule, which is never local input data. | External dependency. |
| 10 | TCS credited in the hands of other person will be allowed to taxpayer only if that other person declares the same in schedule TCS of their ITR | External dependency / not locally verifiable | Same as #9. | External dependency. |
| 11 | In Schedule CG, Table E, the entire loss should be set off with gains available for set off | Indirect calculator/schema/builder coverage — proof pending | The CG-loss basket in `app/engine/schedules/loss_setoff/cyla.py` (STCG/LTCG pools, e.g. lines ~199-280) is allocated the same greedy, ceiling-respecting way as the HP basket in rule #7 — a CG loss cannot be left un-set-off while a matching gain pool remains. | Algorithmic guarantee; not individually re-verified line-by-line for every CG sub-bucket in this pass. |
| 12 | TDS section code such as 194Q,194C,194R or 194M is selected under Schedule TDS2 | Warning not emitted — advisory only | `TDS2Entry.tds_section`/`tds_claimed_this_year` are real fields already read by `ITR2-IN-TDS-001/002/005/006/007/010` (`app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py`), so the section code is available for inspection; no advisory flags these specific business-income-indicating codes on ITR-2. | Data exists; scenario-specific advisory not implemented. |
| 13 | Same for Schedule TDS3 | Warning not emitted — advisory only | Same as #12, via `TDS3Entry.tds_section` (checked only for `"192"` at `ITR2-IN-TDS-011`, not 194Q/C/R/M). | Same. |
| 14 | TDS suggests VDA income was derived in FY but return doesn't fully offer it to tax | Warning not emitted — advisory only | `vda_transactions` (income) and `tds2_entries`/`tds3_entries` (TDS by section, e.g. 194S for VDA) are both real, independently populated fields — the cross-check is representable but not implemented. | Data exists; not wired to a warning. |
| 15 | Same for winnings from lotteries/crossword puzzles/races/card games u/s 115BB | Warning not emitted — advisory only | `si_entries` with `section == "115BB"` and TDS section 194B are both representable; not cross-checked for an advisory. | Same pattern. |
| 16 | Same for income from owning and maintaining race horses | Warning not emitted — advisory only | Race-horse income/TDS (194BB) is representable via `si_entries`/TDS schedules; not cross-checked. | Same pattern. |
| 17 | Same for winnings from Online games u/s 115BBJ | Warning not emitted — advisory only | `si_entries` with `section == "115BBJ"` already has a dedicated rule elsewhere (`ITR2-IN-SI-001`, blocking a *different* scenario — disallowed deductions) — the TDS-vs-income cross-check for this rule specifically is not implemented. | Data exists; different rule occupies the same section code today. |
| 18 | For Resident taxpayers, DTAA benefit is not available, and the claim may not be allowed | Warning not emitted — advisory only | Same underlying data and gap as #8 (a more general phrasing of the same advisory). | Duplicate-in-substance of #8. |
| 19 | Form 10EE should be filed with the amount of Income claimed for relief from taxation u/s 89A | Warning not emitted — advisory only | `OSSection89A`/`os_section_89a` (`app/schemas/itr2.py:933-940,1043`) already captures Section 89A notified foreign-retirement-account income by country — the data needed to trigger a Form-10EE reminder (mirroring the Form-10E/89 pattern at rules #5/6) exists but no advisory rule reads it. | Data exists; mechanism proven elsewhere; not applied here. |
| 20 | If Refund in Part B-TTI > 50 Crores then LEI Number and Valid-up-to date are mandatory | Schema-level invariant | `ITR2FilingProfile.validate_conditional_filing_facts` (`app/schemas/itr2.py:241-244`): `if self.refund_due >= Decimal("500000000") and not self.lei_number: raise ValueError(...)` (and the same for `lei_valid_upto_date`). | Schema-level invariant — the bad state cannot even be constructed. |
| 21 | FORM 10F is mandatory for Non residents to claim benefit of preferable (treaty) rate; TRC flag considered 'No' if Form 10F absent | Warning not emitted — advisory only | `TR1Entry.relief_section`/`ResidentialStatus.NON_RESIDENT` and treaty-rate fields (e.g. `rate_as_per_treaty` on the applicable OS/CG entries) are real inputs; there is no `form_10f_filed`/TRC-flag field and no advisory tying non-resident treaty-rate claims to Form 10F. | Representable via existing NR + treaty-rate data; no advisory wired; no dedicated Form-10F/TRC field exists either, so full enforcement would need a new field too. |
| 22 | TDS has been deducted and claimed u/s 194M, but ITR 2 has been filed | Warning not emitted — advisory only | `TDS2Entry.tds_section`/`TDS3Entry.tds_section` can hold `"194M"` and are already read by other rules (e.g. `ITR2-IN-TDS-010/011` check for `"192"` specifically); no advisory flags 194M specifically as inconsistent with filing ITR-2. | Data exists; not wired to a warning. |
| 23 | Linking of Aadhaar and PAN is required to avoid consequences of not linking, per Circular 03/2023 | External dependency / not locally verifiable | Requires live UIDAI/e-filing-portal Aadhaar-PAN linkage status, not present in local input data. | External dependency. |
| 24 | Quoting of Aadhaar in ITR required u/s 139AA; linking required per Circular 03/2023 | External dependency / not locally verifiable | Same as #23 for the linking-status half. The quoting half (`aadhaar_number: Optional[str]`, `app/schemas/itr2.py:176`) is optional at the schema level with no rule requiring its presence — but the rule's substantive compliance concern (linkage) is still external. | Mixed, but the operative check (linkage) is external; the presence-only half would be a `Missing` finding rather than a warning gap, since it's a real local field with no enforcement. |
| 25 | In schedule CG B1, check computation of indexed cost of acquisition = Cost of acquisition × CII(year of sale)/CII(year of acquisition) | Warning not emitted — advisory only | `CGTransaction.indexed_cost` (`app/schemas/itr2.py:356`) is a raw user-entered `Decimal` field, not engine-computed; `app/engine/constants.py`'s CII table exists per CLAUDE.md, but no rule in `app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py` or `calc_rules.py` recomputes and cross-checks `indexed_cost` against it. | Real, checkable scenario (all inputs needed — cost, acquisition/sale year — already exist); no warning or hard check exists. Also flagged as a correctness risk in the Category-A "Additional bugs" section. |
| 26 | Current year losses (STCL & LTCL) should not be more than ZERO if return is filed under 139(4) | Warning not emitted — advisory only | `ReturnFileSection.BELATED_139_4` (`app/schemas/itr1.py:54`) is a real, selectable filing section, and current-year capital losses are fully computed by the engine — but no rule in `app/engine/validators/itr2/` checks this combination (note: `ITR2Input.cf_losses` itself is never read by the calculator per the codebase's own documented note, so any loss-carry-forward behavior for a belated return runs through the calculator's own current-year-loss computation, not a separate user-supplied field). | Representable from existing computed/selected data; not wired to a warning. |

## Additional bugs noticed (not requested, flagging only — not fixed as part of this audit)

These were found incidentally while reading `input_rules.py`/`calc_rules.py`/the calculator/the
ITD builder in full for the rule-mapping work above. They are real, concrete defects (not missing
validators) — listed here for a future fix pass, per this audit's explicit read-only scope.


**From catA_chunk1 (rules cross-referenced while auditing this range):**

1. **`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:890` — Schedule HP `PassThroghIncome` hardcoded to `0`.** `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:428-430` correctly folds Schedule-PTI HP-head income into `result.house_property_income` (so the schedule's own grand total, `TotalIncomeChargeableUnHP`, is numerically right), but the schedule's own `PassThroghIncome` disclosure line item — meant to show specifically how much of that total came via pass-through — is unconditionally `0` regardless of any actual PTI HP income. This is exactly the "schema-valid but wrong number" class of bug CLAUDE.md's own audit methodology warns about (see rule #79 above).

2. **`app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py:401-441` — eight byte-for-byte duplicate validator blocks.** The check "`tx.full_consideration <= 0 and tx.expenditure_on_transfer > 0`" is appended to `results` **eight separate times** in a row under eight different rule IDs (`ITR2-IN-CG-101` through `ITR2-IN-CG-108`), all with identical messages and identical field paths, for the exact same condition on the exact same transaction. This produces eight duplicate validation failures per offending transaction rather than one, which would visibly spam a taxpayer-facing validation report (8 near-identical error messages for a single mistake) and inflates the apparent rule-coverage count without adding real coverage.

3. **`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:2032-2035` — Table F's applicable-rate and DTAA-rate buckets (`ShortTermUnderAppRate`, `ShortTermUnderDTAARate`, `LongTermUnderDTAARate`) are unconditionally hardcoded to zero**, with no data source anywhere in the builder (acknowledged in the function's own docstring, lines 1950-1959, as a deliberate simplification). Combined with finding #169-173 above, any taxpayer with real STCG/LTCG at applicable or DTAA rates will have a Schedule CG Table F that a strict CBDT validator could legitimately flag as inconsistent with Schedule BFLA's corresponding fields.

**From catA_chunk2 (rules cross-referenced while auditing this range):**

1. **Schedule OS `TotDeductions` omits interest expense u/s 57(1).** `app/engine/itd/itr2.py:1104-1111` computes `total_deductions = deduction_57iia + deductions.expenses + deductions.depreciation` but never adds `deductions.interest_expense_us57`, even though that same value is separately placed into the sibling `IntExp57` field of the identical JSON block. Any taxpayer claiming interest expenditure under Section 57(1) (e.g. against dividend or machinery-rental income) will have it silently excluded from the Schedule OS `TotDeductions` total that CBDT rule #191 requires, understating disclosed deductions relative to the individual line items shown.

2. **`OSRaceHorseActivity.balance` is trusted as raw user input, not recomputed.** Neither `app/schemas/itr2.py:1009-1019` (no `@model_validator`) nor the calculator (`app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:458-459`) nor the builder (`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:1259`) ever recompute `balance` from `receipts - deduction_us57 + amount_not_deductible_us58 + profit_chargeable_us59`. Since `max(_ZERO, balance)` is added directly to taxable other-sources income, an inconsistent `balance` value directly changes tax liability without any cross-check.

3. **Racehorse profit never actually flows through Schedule CYLA/BFLA.** `_schedule_cyla`'s and `_schedule_bfla`'s `"OthSrcRaceHorse"` rows are hardcoded to all-zero (`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:370,428`) regardless of the taxpayer's real racehorse profit (which is added directly to `other_sources_income` instead, bypassing these schedules, `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:458-459`). This makes several official CYLA/BFLA racehorse-related rows (and the statutory requirement that current-year OS loss be set off against racehorse profit first, CBDT rule #267) structurally unimplementable as written, not just untested.

4. **No current-year "Other Sources loss (excluding racehorse)" can ever be represented.** Every component the calculator folds into `other_sources_income` is floored at `max(_ZERO, ...)` before being summed (`app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:433-539`), so a negative OS result (e.g. Section 56(2)(ii) letting-of-machinery loss where deductions exceed rent) is silently discarded rather than being carried into Schedule CYLA's loss-set-off machinery. Real income that could statutorily be set off against other heads under Section 71 is instead just lost from the return; `app/engine/schedules/loss_setoff/cyla.py:46-65`'s `CYLAInput` has no field to carry such a loss at all.

5. **`TaxAccumulatedBalRecPF` totals can silently disagree with their own detail rows.** `TotalIncomeBenefit`/`TotalTaxBenefit` (`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:1044-1045`) are populated from the independent scalars `os_pf_income_benefit`/`os_pf_tax_benefit` rather than summed from the `os_pf_accumulated_entries` detail rows built into the same JSON object one line earlier — the two are never reconciled anywhere, so the filed JSON's own header total and its own line items can disagree.

6. **`Section80CCH` has no statutory ceiling despite CBDT's own validation catalog specifying one (60% of salary).** `app/engine/schedules/deductions/section_80cch.py` allows the full user-claimed amount with no cap of any kind beyond the aggregate GTI ceiling shared with 80CCD(2) (`app/engine/schedules/deductions/__init__.py:143-150`). If the 60%-of-salary cap is a genuine current-law limit (Agniveer Corpus Fund contributions are statutorily linked to a percentage of "Seva Nidhi" pay), this is a real under-validation that could let an excessive claim reach the filed return.

7. **`section80QQB`/`section80RRB` are captured on the frontend draft but never reach `ITR2Input`.** `app/schemas/return_draft.py:1272-1276` defines `section80QQB`/`section80QQBRoyaltyIncome`/`section80RRB` fields, and `app/engine/importers/filed_return_parser.py:645-646` even parses them back out of an already-filed return — but `app/engine/draft_to_itr2_input.py` never reads any of these fields when building `ITR2Input`, and `Chapter6ADeductions` has no corresponding field to receive them. A taxpayer who fills in a Section 80QQB/80RRB (royalty/patent-royalty) claim on the frontend will have it silently vanish before the return is ever computed or filed.

## Summary of classification counts (rules 191–382, 191 rules)

- Explicit validator executed: 24
- Indirect calculator/schema/builder coverage — proof pending: 92
- Schema-level invariant: 1 (dedicated) + 2 rules that also qualify alongside "Indirect" (#353, #355)
- Missing — genuine gap: 56
- Not representable / out of scope: 18
- External dependency / not locally verifiable: 0

**From catA_chunk3 (rules cross-referenced while auditing this range):**

1. **Section 90/90A vs Section 91 foreign-tax relief is conflated.** `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:1146-1149` sums `tr1_entries[*].relief_claimed` into a single `r.relief_90_91` figure regardless of `relief_section`. `app/engine/itd/itr2.py:3968-3969` then reports the *entire* combined figure under `"Section90"` and hardcodes `"Section91": 0`. Any taxpayer with genuine Section 91 (unilateral, non-DTAA) relief will have it misreported as Section 90/90A relief, and `"Section91"` will always read zero. The grand total (`TotTaxRelief`) is still numerically correct, but the required split (rules #526/#527) is wrong. Affects rules 526, 527.

2. **`result.total_tcs` omits spouse/other-person-claimed TCS.** `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:1163` computes `r.total_tcs = sum(entry.tcs_credit_claimed for entry in input_data.tcs_entries)`, never including `entry.tcs_credit_claimed_spouse_or_other`. This feeds Part B-TTI's `"TCS"` figure (`itd/itr2.py:4027`), while Schedule TCS's own declared `"TotalSchTCS"` (`itd/itr2.py:3792-3799`) correctly sums both own-hand and spouse-claimed amounts. The two totals will diverge for any return with spouse-claimed TCS under section 5A. Affects rules 460, 493.

3. **AMT/AMTC credit chain is largely unwired into Part B-TTI.** Several Part B-TTI fields that are supposed to be sourced from Schedule AMT/AMTC are hardcoded to `0` regardless of the actual computed AMT figures: `tax_payable_deemed_total_income = 0` (`itd/itr2.py:3957`, feeds items 1a-1d and `GrossTaxPayable`'s first operand), `"CreditUS115JD": 0` (`itd/itr2.py:3990`), `"TaxPayAfterCreditUs115JD": 0` (`itd/itr2.py:3991`), and `"DeemedIncomeUs115JC": 0` in Part B-TI (`itd/itr2.py:3885`, never wired to Schedule AMT's own `AdjustedUnderSec115JC`). Separately, Schedule AMTC itself (`_schedule_amtc`) only tracks the *brought-forward* AMT credit ledger — it never computes or records the *current year's own newly generated* AMT credit (Sl.3 = Sl.2-Sl.1 per rule #424/#425). Together these make every AMT-dependent Part-B-TTI/Part-B-TI/Schedule-AMTC cross-check in this chunk (rules 422, 423, 424, 425, 511, 517, 518, 519, 522, 540, 544, 545) structurally unsatisfiable for any real AMT-applicable return, though most of them are individually self-documented as known gaps in the code's own comments. Affects rules 422, 423, 424, 425, 511, 517, 518, 519, 522, 540, 544, 545.

4. **Schedule ESOP total is never wired into Part B-TTI.** `"GrossTaxPay": {"TaxInc17": 0, "TaxDeferred17": 0, "TaxDeferredPayableCY": 0}` is hardcoded in `_partb_tti()` (`itd/itr2.py:3989`) with no reference at all to `_schedule_esop()`'s real, correctly-computed deferred-tax totals (`total_attributed`/`total_balance_cf`, `itd/itr2.py:3600-3616`). Unlike the AMT gaps above, this one carries no acknowledging code comment. Affects rules 481, 545.

5. **Capital-gains figures reported in Part B-TI / Schedule CYLA use a different netting basis than Schedule CG's own Table E.** Table E (`_cg_loss_setoff_table`, `itd/itr2.py:1407-1516`) reflects only the intra-Schedule-CG (section 70) set-off, sourced from `cyla.cg_intra_head_remaining`/`cg_gross_income`. But Part B-TI's `CapGain` sub-items (`stcg_20`, `stcg_30`, `ltcg_112`, etc., `itd/itr2.py:3816-3824`) are sourced from `post_loss_cg`, which reflects *additional* CYLA cross-head absorption (e.g. HP loss set off against STCG) *and* BFLA brought-forward absorption on top of the intra-CG netting. Similarly, Schedule CYLA's own per-bucket `IncOfCurYrUnderThatHead` (`itd/itr2.py:344-349`) uses the *raw pre-intra-CG-netting* gross income (`cg_gross_income`), not the post-Table-E remaining figure the form expects entering CYLA. The three schedules (Schedule CG/Table E, Schedule CYLA, Part B-TI) can therefore legitimately disagree on the exact same STCG/LTCG bucket whenever any cross-head loss is set off — this does not affect the final tax liability (which is computed independently from `result` fields, not from these JSON sub-items), but it is a genuine cross-schedule disclosure inconsistency against the CBDT rules requiring these figures to reconcile. Affects rules 496, 497, 565, 566, 567, 568 (and adjacent to 489-491, which reconcile internally but not against Table E).

6. **DTAA-rate short-term and long-term capital gains are never surfaced in Part B-TI.** `"ShortTermSplRateDTAA": 0` and `"LongTermSplRateDTAA": 0` are hardcoded in `_partb_tti()`'s sibling `_partb_ti()` (`itd/itr2.py:3856,3861`); `_post_loss_cg_baskets()` (`calculators/itr2.py:320-366`) has no `stcg_dtaa`/output key for a DTAA-rate CG bucket at all, even though the CYLA/BFLA engines internally track `stcg_dtaa`/`ltcg_dtaa` baskets. A taxpayer with genuine DTAA-rate capital gains would have this income silently absent from Part B-TI's disclosure. Affects rules 498, 499.

7. **Section 87A eligibility may be over-restrictive for RNOR taxpayers.** `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py:1118` computes `is_resident = residential_status == ResidentialStatus.RESIDENT`, excluding `NOT_ORDINARILY_RESIDENT` (RNOR) from Section 87A rebate eligibility. Per the code's own adjacent comment at line ~390 ("NOR is a species of resident under section 6 — only a non-resident is excluded"), RNOR is legally still "resident" for most purposes, and the CBDT rule text for #535 itself explicitly lists "Resident or Resident but not Ordinarily Resident Individual" as eligible for the >5L-old-regime restriction (implying RNOR is otherwise eligible for 87A up to that point) — suggesting RNOR individuals may be wrongly denied the rebate entirely by this stricter gate. Not independently confirmed against a live scenario; flagged for further investigation. Adjacent to rules 533, 535.

**From catA_chunk4 (rules cross-referenced while auditing this range):**

1. **Schedule 80D silently fabricates "Not Provided" insurer/policy data instead of rejecting** — `_policy_insurance_details()` (`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:2371-2372`) substitutes the literal string `"Not Provided"` for a missing `insurer_name`/`policy_number` when a claim is emitted to the CBDT JSON. Beyond failing CBDT rules #610-614, this risks a *live* ITD rejection or defect notice, since the official JSON would carry a fabricated placeholder string rather than real evidentiary data.
2. **Schedule 80D per-policy breakup can silently disagree with the claimed aggregate** — `_schedule_80d()` (`app/engine/itd/itr2.py:2396-2419`) emits `"TotalPayments"` from the flat `self_premium`/`parents_premium` scalar inputs, never from `sum(row.HealthInsAmt for row in ...)`. A taxpayer (or a buggy frontend) could submit `policies` rows that don't sum to the claimed premium and the builder would emit an internally inconsistent Schedule 80D without complaint.
3. **ITR-2 is missing the ₹35,00,000 Section 80EE loan-principal ceiling that ITR-1 and ITR-4 already enforce for the identical field shape** — compare `app/engine/validators/itr1/input_rules.py:2405-2408` and `app/engine/validators/itr4/input_rules.py:2700-2705`/`4043-4048` (both raise/fail when `total_loan_amount > 3,500,000`) against `app/engine/validators/itr2/input_rules.py`, which has no such check anywhere despite `ITR2Input.loan_details_80ee_list` carrying the same `total_loan_amount` field. This looks like an accidental omission during ITR-2's build-out rather than a deliberate scope decision.
4. **User-entered `indexed_cost` on `CGTransaction` is never cross-checked against the statutory CII formula** (relevant to Category B/D rule #25 below, but noted here as a correctness risk, not just an advisory gap) — `indexed_cost: Decimal` (`app/schemas/itr2.py:356`) is a raw, unclamped user input rather than an engine-computed value, and nothing in `app/engine/validators/itr2/` or `app/engine/calculators/itr2.py` verifies it equals `cost_of_acquisition * CII(year of sale)/CII(year of acquisition)`. A materially wrong indexed cost — whether from taxpayer error or a bad import — would flow straight into the LTCG computation without any internal check.

**From catBD (Category B/D pass):**

See the Category-A result file's bugs section for the four findings from that pass. One
additional note specific to this B/D pass: `TR1Entry.form67_filed` (`app/schemas/itr2.py:570`)
and `OSSection89A`/`os_section_89a` (`app/schemas/itr2.py:933-940,1043`) are both fields that
appear to have been added *specifically* to support the Form-67 (#3) and Form-10EE (#19)
advisories respectively — the data modeling work for these two warnings looks complete, but the
actual `Severity.D` rule emitting them (the same one-line pattern already used for
`ITR2-IN-FORM-001`/`002`) was never added. These two are the lowest-effort, highest-confidence
fixes in this entire B/D list, since the mechanism, the rule-ID convention, and the source field
all already exist — only the `_result(..., Severity.D)` call itself is missing.
